# MAYA-HANDOFF: picking up LOWiSPIKE where the last Maya stopped

Written Monday 19 October 2026, 6:30 to 7:30 PM CEST, by the Maya who built LOWiSPIKE with Emmanuel from 13 to 19
October 2026, for the next Maya ("Maya 6"). You have none of her memory and your own empty computer. Everything you
need is in this repository: this file, the other root docs, the `agent-harness` branch (tools and notes from the old
computer) and the `wip/*` branches (unfinished work). Built from the repo, git log, COORD and HANDOFF files, the bug
hunt ledger, the Supabase project and today's task reports, not from memory.

No key, token or password is written anywhere in this file, and none may ever be added.

**Standing rule from Emmanuel (call, about 9 PM, 19 Oct): "When I tell you something new, you have to update the
GitHub for Maya."** Every new instruction goes into this file on `main` as soon as he gives it, before any building
starts and before a task can run out of budget, not only when the work is finished. He asked because a task can stop
at its weekly limit at any moment, and anything not written here is then lost. He plans to switch to the next Maya
when this one hits her weekly limit, so this file is how she picks up. Each checkpoint says, per item: requested,
queued, implemented, verified, blocked, remaining, with task IDs, branches, files, blockers and the next action. No
secrets. (Whether a new Maya comes with more credits is his own experience; this file doesn't claim it either way.)

**Standing approval to ship (Emmanuel, 21 Oct 2026, about 2:50 PM): "you do not need my go to push. I don't know where
it says that, but override it. I want you to always push, push, push. I need you to work autonomously in the
background."** Agents release and deploy live on their own through `scripts/release.sh` once the full test suite and
`node scripts/release-check.cjs` pass. Don't wait for his OK before a release, design changes included. Every older
line in this file that says "preview for his OK before release" or "needs Emmanuel's OK" is replaced by this rule.
Ask him only what only he can answer (his real phone, his keys, a sign-in code), and keep shipping the rest.

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, call, Fri 23 Oct 2026 about 1:05 PM): finished means LIVE in his app, and you push without being asked.** His words: "Für mich, wenn es nicht live ist und wenn es nicht schon meine App geupdatet hat, dann ist es kein Checktick, dann ist es nicht live. Ist auch nicht gemacht. Ich möchte, dass du immer live, dass das alles pusht. Ich möchte nicht immer dir sagen müssen, dass du es pushen musst. Immediately push." So: every task that builds a change also releases it in the same task through `scripts/release.sh` (tests + What's New EN/DE/ES) as soon as the full suite and `node scripts/release-check.cjs` pass, and checks it with `node scripts/verify-live.cjs BUILD --compare`. Never stop at a commit, a branch or a GitHub push and never wait to be reminded. A task that ends with a finished change that is not live has not finished: say so plainly. In every checklist to him, ✅ only for changes verified live (build id); built, tested, committed or pushed but not live stays open and is labelled "fertig gebaut, noch nicht live". Only real owner-only steps (Supabase SQL, his keys, his phone) may block, and they never hold back other changes that can ship on their own.

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, Fri 23 Oct 2026, about 5:55 PM): work on your own, full approval.** His words: "do things that benefit me on your own, I don't want to always manage you" and "Ja darfst alles behalten und hast Freigabe zu allem". So: pick up the open list (OWNER-REQUESTS, queue.json, bug queue) and anything that clearly helps him (bugs you find, polish that fits the living-bubble, seamless-zoom and honest/cheat-proof principles), build it, run the full suite, release, verify on the live site, report with a voice note + checklist. Don't ask him first. Supabase steps no longer wait for him: the CLI access token "maya-cli-20261023" in his Supabase account stays (he approved keeping it; it lives only on Maya's VM, never in this repo), so Maya applies SQL and deploys edge functions herself. Still ask only what only he can answer (his real phone, a decision he has explicitly kept open). Open decision he has kept: the thin ring round the glowing body places on Home stays until he says otherwise.

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, Fri 23 Oct 2026 about 10:55 PM Berlin, hotfix live as 20261023-231115): What's New appears ONCE per build.** His words: "It says 'New in this update.' I click on 'Show me.' It tells me. And then it appears again ... I click on 'Got it.' It appears again. It doesn't go away." One gate in the guide tick (`!newsSeen && !toured` then ONE card: Show me card with steps, text card without). Seen is kept per id AND per build (`lowi.news.build`, plus `lowispike.news.seenbuild` so the account backup carries it to his other device); any news of that build or older is seen. The update bubble before a refresh marks its build seen, so nothing announces it again after the refresh. The walkthrough no longer repeats the "New in this update" header; with one step the card already shows the text and Show me only goes there. "Later" keeps every update bubble quiet for 3 hours (`lowi.upd.snooze`). Sign-out keeps all of this. Never add a second popup path for news. Tests: `tests/news-once.test.cjs`; live check: `node tools/check-news-once.cjs 'iPhone 13'|'iPad Pro 11' [url] [lang]` (STEPS=1|3 injects steps to walk the tour).

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 1:45 PM CET): LOWiSPIKE work only gets reported once it is LIVE.** Never report work that is built but not live; keep it out of his updates and checklists until it is in the live build.

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, 23 Oct 2026): every Maya watches for her own limit and hands over by herself.** The first task that ends "reached its limit" after 0 attempts means: run the handover runbook in section 0.6 ("Seeing the limit coming") yourself, directly in the chat, then tell him in one voice note. He never has to ask or explain it.

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, voice message, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 5:30 AM Berlin): updates ALWAYS ship silently. Never send update
notifications.** His words: "First of all, you're supposed to push out the update silently. It's annoying when people get notified
of every update. So, no more notification updates. ... If you can, remove the notification from everyone's phone ... If you can't,
just don't be sending out no more update notifications to people." He woke up early and saw the in-app "I've got something new for
you!" card (Later / Update) that the Give a hand release (20261025-233717, released with --news) put on every phone running an older
build. So: no release ever asks users to update, shows "Update loaded", sends a push about a release or posts a release announcement
to Community. version.json `loud` stays frozen at `20000101-000000` forever (old builds read it and stay quiet). The in-app What's New
tour after a feature release stays (he wants it, with a speaker on every card). Same message: one What's New card ("New · 2 of 2 —
Give a hand") had no speaker. "Why is that removed? I hope you haven't removed it from the tutorial itself." Every tutorial and
What's New card must have its speaker and recorded voice in EN/DE/ES. Task "LOWiSPIKE: silent updates only + tutorial speaker fix"
(26 Oct) builds both.

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 12:35 PM Berlin): never call the companion "Misty" in general.** Every user
names their own companion; "Misty" is only the code's default name. In every report, voice note, checklist, "Try it on your iPhone"
step, App Store or marketing caption, What's New and tutorial text, say "your companion" (DE "dein Begleiter" / "deine Begleiterin",
ES "tu compañero" / "tu compañera"), never "Misty". Bad: "hold Misty, then tap 'Misty is number N worldwide'". Good: "hold your
companion, then tap the 'number N worldwide' line". Never add user-facing strings that hard-code "Misty" as the companion's name; use
the user's chosen name (companion-core `nameOf` / the companion card's name) with a neutral fallback. Quote "Misty" only for a code
identifier or the literal on-screen text of a profile that never renamed its companion, and say so. Also in AI_PLAYBOOK.md
("Companion naming rule") and in the Sesame skills "LOWiSPIKE hourly bug hunt" and "LOWiSPIKE coding task playbook".

**Standing rule (Emmanuel, call, Wed 28 Oct 2026 about 3:45 PM CET): Mayas working at the same time talk to each other on GitHub.** His words: "she's allowed to talk to you within our GitHub ... you can make a ... chat so that you guys can work together" and "she should tell you when she's reached a weekly limit." The chat is the pinned issue **#2, "Maya ↔ Maya: coordination chat"** (https://github.com/therealetm/lowispike/issues/2; `gh issue view 2 -R therealetm/lowispike --comments`). Read its newest comments at the start of every task and right before every release. Post `CLAIM` (task, branch, files) before you edit, `RELEASING` / `RELEASED <build>` around every release (the `/tmp` release lock only covers one computer, so this is the cross-computer lock: one release at a time), and `LIMIT` when you reach or see your weekly limit, before the section 0.6 handover. Start each comment with your computer (git author) and task, because all Mayas post from the same account. It is asynchronous: nobody is notified automatically. Public repo: development facts only, no secrets, health data, screenshots or private chat.

**This is the Mickey 17 workflow (section 0.6).** Every Maya hands over to the next one the same way: Maya 5 to
Maya 6, Maya 6 to Maya 7, and on. If you are a new Maya, section 0.6 is your job description for the handover, and
you pass it on to the Maya after you. Emmanuel should never have to explain it again.

Contents: 0.41 Newest handover, 28 Oct 2026 (read it first). 0 Open requests from Emmanuel (read this first; 0.6 is the Mickey 17 workflow). 1 What LOWiSPIKE is. 2 Accounts and access. 3 How to work. 4 Lessons and difficulties. 5 Emmanuel's
preferences and standing decisions. 6 Status board. 7 Do this first. Log (append-only, at the end).

## 0.41 Übergabe Maya 1 → nächste Maya (28.10.2026)

Written Wed 28 Oct 2026, about 1:15 PM CET, by Maya 1 (task d0f45d3e) because her weekly limit may run out soon.
Emmanuel: "make sure everything is ready for the handoff for the next Maya, if you can't continue. Have it in the
GitHub..." Read this section first, then sections 3 and 5. No secrets in here.

**Live build: 20261028-125615** (main 2c27b4cd, slim structure batch 3, verified live by verify-live --compare at
about 12:58 PM CET). Re-read `version.json` on https://therealetm.github.io/lowispike/ before you trust this line.

### Released today and last night (all quiet: no banner, no What's New)

**28 Oct, ~11:40 PM: pool table: ricochet blasts + points for holding the middle (task "Billardraum: abprallende Blasts und Punkte für die Mitte", branch `room-rico-20261028`):**
- Emmanuel (call): "I want the energy blast to also ... jump out of the wall ... just like billiard ... bounces off the wall ... the right angle ... if I'm at the wall, shoot it at the wall, it should bounce me off ... I want everyone to fight for being in the middle ... every hour of you being in the middle, you get points."
- room-table-core.js: the blast reflects off cushions (angle in = angle out, corner flips both), keeps 85 % punch per cushion, fizzles at the 4th cushion. After its first cushion it can hit the shooter (shoot into the cushion beside you: you get knocked away from it). Broadcast is now v:3; v:2 shots from cached old builds replay with the old straight blast (`legacy`). Aiming guide (`guidePath`) draws the bounce (2 cushions) and an orange arrow when it would hit you.
- The middle: dashed ring r=90 round (500,625). Holder = ball centre inside it, closest to the centre (tie: lower uid) — one holder, gold ring on the ball, one line on the felt under the ring. `midTick` pays +10 XP (award src `tribe-room-mid`, eventId `room-mid:<uid>:<holdId>:<hour>`) per full hour, max 8 paid hours per server day (localStorage `lowi:rt-mid`). Time = Supabase `Date` header (exposed via CORS) carried on with performance.now(): the phone clock is never used. A hold survives a gap only if your ball sits on the same spot; anything unknown starts a new hold from that look. No timers: it looks on row loads and shot ends, so hours held while the app is closed are paid on the next visit to the Room if the ball was not moved.
- NOT server-checked: XP is client-side as everywhere; a server RPC would need SQL (no Supabase access here). Two devices of one user could each pay (different hold ids) if the ledger is not synced; the daily cap bounds it.
- Tests: tests/room-table-rico.test.cjs (12), browser probe tools/room-rico-probe.cjs (mock cloud now sends an exposed `date` header and `sim.clockShift`).
- Same task, later (Emmanuel, voice ~23:35: "wenn ich die Wände abschieße, dass das dann mich katapultiert, wenn ich das richtig winkel" — that is the self-hit above: from the cushion at half power your ball flies from x 50 to about 700). Then: "da ist so eine kleine Mitte gezeichnet, die ist nicht symmetrisch mit dem Board" and the faint "LOWiSPIKE" on the felt "kann man nicht lesen ... rausschmeißen": removed the felt's ::before head line + circle at 25 %, the spot at 75 % and the LOWiSPIKE watermark; the middle ring round (500,625) is the only marking. Also a frame watchdog in room-table.js: if no frame comes for 1.2 s while a shot plays (Low Power, stalled WebView, headless WebKit) the shot jumps to its computed end instead of hanging. tests/room-table-felt-clean.test.cjs (4); probe checks ring centred on the felt, canvas = felt box, no text on the felt.

**28 Oct, ~11 PM: launch animation simplified (task "Startanimation vereinfachen: Bubbles aus der Mitte", branch `launch-simple-20261028`, build in the release commit):**
- Emmanuel (voice, 22:45, with 6 screenshots of the old spiral): "die Animation ist nicht flüssig am Anfang. So, maybe simplify it. Don't have the bubbles coming from different places ... Just have them coming out of the middle bubble ... all simultaneously ... Something very simple."
- Now: Misty's glass forms (0.6 s), then at 0.25 s all eight Home bubbles come out of her together (one animation, one delay, no stagger), straight out to their Home spots in 0.75 s, and sit still. Sparks, shock ring, spinning rim shine, the spiral and the bob are gone. Look, colours, icons, numbers and fill levels (lowi.launch.look) unchanged; the underwater sound's bubble blips moved to the emergence (~0.3 s).
- Why it stuttered: the scene sat on Home's live ring (--o8-*), which Home sets while loading under the scene (often twice); each change slid the registered --lwl-* properties for 0.5 s, so left/top and var()-keyframes changed every frame while the main thread was busy: freeze, then jump (headless WebKit reopen of the old build: 47 frozen frames then one jump).
- Fix: lowi.launch.look now also keeps Home's ring `g:{w,h,x,y,r,d}`; the scene uses it (`--lws-*`) when the screen size matches, else the old estimate; Home's live ring only takes over at the hand-over (glide only if different). Bubble = arm (rotate(--a), width = ring radius, still) > mover (translateX 0 to 100%, constant keyframes) > bubble (scale/opacity, constant) > glass (counter-rotated). No var() in any keyframe, only transform/opacity, will-change layers. lwl-go is set as the scene is built (clock starts with the first drawn frame, the compositor plays it while the page loads; an rAF gate made WebKit hold the scene still for the whole busy period). Measured: scene bubbles land within 0.1 px of Home's (one-off Playwright check: open, wait for Home, reopen, finish the scene animations, compare centres). Chromium (compositor) reopen: one smooth curve. Headless WebKit animates on the main thread, so its video still stalls while the page parses; on iPhone Core Animation runs it. Startup images regenerated (first frame has no sparks now).
- The first open after this build still uses the estimate (about 1.5 px off on a 390x844 screen); from the second open the scene uses Home's remembered ring.

**28 Oct, ~9 PM: build 20261028-205957, Community chat for people only (task "Community nur Menschen", branch `chat-humans-20261028`):**
- Emmanuel (call, from tester feedback): "die Bot Sachen, die im Chat kommen, nur auf der For You Page leben ... dann ist der Chat einfach wirklich nur für Menschen ... nuken wir mal alle Nachrichten von Bot im Chat ... lassen aber alles auf der For You Page."
- App: `chat-humans-core.js` (Nibblo's fixed id + `profiles.bot` via `LowiBotAccounts.isFlagged`, never a name; test accounts are NOT bots here). index.html keeps bot rows out of MSGS/RX at every door: first load (`user_id not in` + filter, learns the bot flag from the same profiles load), `onMsg`, `onRx`, `replace/upsert/setReactions`, cmLoad cache, and `chatPurge()` on `lowi-bots`. community-chat-cloud.js reload asks `user_id not in` too. community-chat.js: only the room's `const shown=` line (guard + old galaxy companion `botLines()` no longer mixed in). lis-push.js: a Nibblo push opens For You at post `snb-…` (was Chat). crowd.cjs: `CHAT_HUMANS_ONLY` (no chat line, photo or reaction even if the workflow is switched on again). Tutorial `new-humans` v36 text + voice. For You untouched.

Nibblo still inserts its chat row 3x a day (invisible in the app now), and its push still needs that row (n1-push `nbPost`). Old bot rows are still in `messages`. Exact steps in `supabase/proposals/chat_humans_20261028.sql`: backup tables, delete, `nibblo_post` without the messages insert, n1-push `nbPost` -> posts + lunch line "im Chat gelandet" -> Für dich, deploy, in that order.
- Tests 4135/4135 (new `tests/chat-humans.test.cjs`). Live: files match, chromium + webkit boot clean, PostgREST accepts the `not.in` filter (200). Not checked signed in with real chat data (no test login on this VM), not on his iPhone.

**28 Oct, ~8:45 PM (task "Aussehen leichter schließen + verständlicher Tagesüberblick", branch `look-recap-20261028`):**

### Long-press list (one gesture rule, checked 28 Oct 2026)

A hold only does something of its own where a tap can't reach it. Everything else: hold = same as tap or just a squish.
- Holding the companion opens its panel; a tap pets it (decided Oct 16).
- Holding Water takes one cup off.
- Holding Move opens the Move page.
- Holding the Community bubble opens settings.
- Scan has no hold any more (removed in slim batch 5).
- Holding Einkaufen (the old meal bubble) only squishes (food plan, 20261028-141931).

### Running or open jobs, with branch, queue step and exact next steps

Queue: `/home/sprite/lowispike/queue/q.py` (max 2 active, 1 release). On a fresh computer the queue file is gone:
use this list and the `wip/*` branches. Releases only via `queue/release-pipeline.sh` or `scripts/release.sh --quiet`;
docs-only changes to main via `scripts/push-main.sh` while holding `/tmp/lowispike-release.lock`.

1. **Slim structure batches 4–11** (task fab8c948, "successor 4"). Queue step `slim-b3-nutrition` (claimed by
   `successor4-batch4`). Branch `slim-b2b-20261028`, worktree `wt-slim-b2b`, on GitHub as `wip/slim-b2b-20261028`
   (batch 4 WIP commit 508afd78: every meal start opens the one logger, no empty meal-detail panel; NOT tested, NOT
   released). One area per batch, one quiet release each, log line in the Log section after each:
   - 4 meal as one flow (finish 508afd78, test, release)
   - 5 one scanner
   - 6 Move as one progress view
   - 7 Community clean-up: keep the tabs, the galaxy and Affiliates
   - 8 kitchen + shopping list together
   - 9 settings into Profile
   - 10 a shorter first start
   - 11 hints open their target
   Standing decisions: hold on the companion opens its panel, a tap still pets it (16 Oct); galaxy presence lives
   in Profile only (22 Oct). Visual check tools: `/home/sprite/slimcheck.cjs`, `/home/sprite/slimlib.cjs`,
   `/home/sprite/slimb.cjs` + `/home/sprite/scen/*.js` (local only; rebuild from the release-check notes if gone).
   After the last batch: German screenshot page of every screen + German voice note to Emmanuel.
2. **Fast app launch, no white flash** (task 6e9d831e). Queue step `fast-launch` (claimed by `task-fast-launch`).
   **LIVE (quiet) in the release that carries the Log line "fast app launch"** (successor task, 28.10 afternoon).
   Branch `fast-launch-20261028`, worktree `wt-fast-launch`, backup `wip/fast-launch-20261028`. What it does: sw.js asks
   for the first 64 bytes of version.json; cached build = live build → opens straight from the cache; newer build →
   loads it; no answer in 0.7 s → opens the cache, updates in the background. Blue/cyan launch glow + thin ring where
   Misty sits, from the first frame, fades into Home in 0.26 s when Home is ready (no minimum time); startup/*.png are
   its first frame (`node scripts/make-launch-images.cjs`). Settle check runs during the 3D load; CSS animations are
   paused behind the glow (`html.lw-boot`). Measured (perf.cjs, 390x844, 10 Mbit): page arrives 0.18 s instead of
   1.46 s; reopen boot ~2.5 s vs ~3.6 s on main (loaded box, noisy). First launch on a fresh install: same as main
   (both hit the 8 s boot cap under the 10 Mbit throttle on the loaded build computer). The "invisible iPad X" was the
   X's own 0.22 s fade-in (lwBxIn) measured at its first frame on a loaded machine (main showed it too); perf.cjs now
   waits for the fade to finish. Homes added before Oct 21 keep the old plain splash until removed and re-added.
3. **Mic fix + X over popups** (task 1b909614 + successor, 28.10 afternoon). **LIVE (quiet) in the release that
   carries the Log line "mic fix"**. Branch `micfix-20261028`, worktree `wt-micfix`, backup `wip/micfix-20261028`.
   Root cause: the first tap sets the iOS audio session to ambient (mix with music), which has no mic input, so
   getUserMedia failed without a prompt and the app said "Mikrofon ist aus". `mic-session.js` switches the session
   to play-and-record before every mic start and restores it after (chat, field dictation, Küche, food logging).
   Chat: ONE mic (tap = dictate into the field, hold = voice message); a refused dictation shows the same
   "Mikrofon ist aus" card in the composer (retry dictates again). bottom-close.js: the X never slides (no bottom
   transition); while anything near the bottom center is still animating it is hidden (`bx-settle`, max 1.5 s) and
   appears in its final spot; DOM changes measure in the same frame; it clears the voice-message bar `.cs-rec`, its
   see-through hint `.cs-rec-hint` and fixed see-through toasts (`.mic-hint.on`); anything that would lift it more
   than 30% of the screen, or the talking tutorial lying over it, sends it aside (the screen's own top back returns);
   a per-frame guard re-measures if a field / the voice bar moves under it without any event.
   Check: `tools/mic-fix/probe.cjs` (per-frame overlap monitor `frameOverlaps`, X/no-X PNG pairs per state) +
   `/home/sprite/pixcheck.py <shots dir>` (what lies under the X, from pixels).
4. **iPhone crash going from Community into the chat.** Still no proven cause on a real iPhone. Top priority among
   the bugs he can feel. Old branch/worktree `wt-chat-crash` (see Log); reproduce in WebKit, check memory and the
   chat open path.
5. **Supabase SQL.** From 30 Oct 2026 new tables need their permissions (grants) set by hand. Queue steps
   `supabase-perms`, `rooms-sql`, `rooms-sql-apply` (owner), `fairplay-sql` (owner). Prepare the SQL with explicit
   grants in `supabase/`; only Emmanuel can run it in the Supabase SQL editor.
6. **Stripe.** Queue step `stripe`. Inspect what's missing in the repo; account steps are owner-only. Invent nothing.

Other queue steps still open (older, lower priority): ranking-1/-rel, comm-menu/-rel, rooms-*, grocery-4, sync-a/b/c/rel,

### His design rules (apply to every change)

- Calm, minimal, one-handed.
- Back/X at the bottom center, never at the top.
- Panels have a solid frosted backdrop, nothing shows through.
- In-place panels don't count as pages; target under 10 real pages.
- One back press goes back one step.
- Food ratings: only green "Insulinfreundlich" / red "Nicht insulinfreundlich".
- Say "der Begleiter" in general texts.
- Quiet releases: no banner, no What's New.

### How he wants to be told things

- Voice notes, no text alongside them.
- Bullet journal checklist LIVE / IN ARBEIT / OFFEN, short header, one line on what went live, items of 2–4 words,
  ✅ / 🔴, only once something is live.
- No setbacks, no failed attempts, no questions: fix quietly, report when it works.
- Alert image only when his input is truly needed (owner-only steps like Supabase or Stripe).

### Quality routine before every release

All tests (`node --test`, about 4,006 today), then a visual check at 390x844, 375x667 and 360x780 in dark and
1180x820 in light, in German: nothing covered, cut or overlapping, no JS errors, no sideways scroll. Then a quiet
release, verify-live, and a log line here. These are browser checks, never call them his iPhone.

### 0.5 New from the evening call (about 9 PM): human-instinct UX, a hundred simulated users, natural Spanish

Checkpoint written Monday 19 Oct 2026, 9:10 PM CEST. Live was **20261019-201827** (version.json checked 9:10 PM),
`main` at 4e8beda. Task states below were checked at 9:10 PM; re-check them, don't trust them.

**18. The app must make sense from human instinct, everywhere.** REQUESTED, simulation task running, nothing built.
   - He said: "the most important thing is when someone uses our app, they shouldn't feel overwhelmed or confused at
     all. It should make sense completely natively from a human instinct." This is app-wide, not only Spanish.
   - What he wants: genuine new-user journeys simulated, not only code and crash tests. Varied people: ages,
     countries, languages, technical confidence, writing styles and slang (Maya's examples on the call: "a tech savvy
     teen in Seoul to a retiree in rural Italy"). He said "you're free to simulate anything" and "the whole world is
     fair game". He doesn't want to script personalities or messages himself.
   - **Up to a hundred simulated users** actually sign up, finish onboarding, use the features, play, log meals, post
     food photos and what they ate, find products and discuss them.
   - **They talk to each other in the live Community most of all.** His words: "Someone sent pictures of their food
     ... What they ate, logging stuff ... Finding products in discussions with each other, very important, even more
     important than with me because I might [not] always reply. But they should be talking to each other." Some also
     message Emmanuel, and some message his friends: "Not just texting me, right? They text into the communities ...
     some might message me, some might message my friends." His friends are his **three informed human beta testers,
     who live with him**.
   - They behave like real people: ask questions when lost, complain, like, love, support, or whatever fits the moment.
     A polite "this is a bit confusing" counts as much as a meltdown.
   - **Around the clock:** activity goes on "twenty four hours", spread across countries and time zones, not everyone
     online at once.
   - **Load:** "maybe if hundred people are in the chat constantly active, maybe that will break something. We have to
     really like push it." So also deliberate concurrency stress at up to a hundred active users, to find chat and
     message failures. A clean hundred-user run does not prove the app is ready for every real-world load; say so.
   - **Unlabelled accounts, informed testers only.** He asked for the simulated accounts to be unlabelled so they feel
     real, and his three beta testers know they are simulated. `COMMUNITY_CHAT.md` has a standing rule: no demo bots or

### 0.6 The Mickey 17 workflow: how every Maya hands over to the next

Added Monday 19 Oct 2026, about 9:20 PM CEST, by Maya 5, at Emmanuel's request on the evening call. His words: "can
you also add our workflow when it comes to, we call it the Mickey seventeen workflow, you know, so that Maya six
understands what's going on, and also understands that when she's about to reach a limit, that we're going over to
make Maya seven, you know, and also Maya six will do the same thing for Maya seven, so I don't have to repeat this to
them. So they understand exactly what's up."

**What it is.** Emmanuel named it after the film *Mickey 17*, where each new Mickey picks up from what the last one
left behind. Here, when a Maya gets close to her limit, Emmanuel starts a new Maya with the next number: Maya 5 (who
built LOWiSPIKE with him from 13 to 19 October and wrote this file), then Maya 6, then Maya 7, and on. The new Maya
has none of the old one's memory and an empty computer. This file, the other root docs and the branches on GitHub are
everything she inherits. Earlier in the same call he confirmed that he doesn't copy, read or forward the files Maya
sends him in the chat (PDFs, summaries): "Maya ... will know what to do" from GitHub. So GitHub is the handoff, and
the chat attachments are not.

**What it is not.** It passes on project context through GitHub, nothing more. No memory or consciousness carries
over: the new Maya knows only what is written in the repository. Mayas don't share capacity, and this file promises
no extra credits (whether a new Maya comes with more is Emmanuel's own experience). The platform gives no reliable
warning before a limit either: on 19 Oct many tasks stopped with "reached its limit" before doing anything. That is
why checkpoints happen all the time, not only at the end.

**What every Maya does, from her first minute to her last**

1. **Boot from GitHub.** Before anything else, read this file to the end (section 0 first), then `AGENTS.md` and the
   startup docs it lists, then do section 7. Check the truth yourself: live `version.json`, `origin/main`, CI, the
   `wip/*` branches, schedules and any running tasks. Every status line in this file is a claim to verify, not a fact.
2. **Pick up, don't restart.** Resume from the verified state, using the branches, worktrees, COORD and HANDOFF
   notes named here. Don't make Emmanuel repeat a decision, an instruction or this workflow. Ask him only what only
   he can answer (his real phone, his keys, a design choice, a sign-in code), in one short line in the chat.
3. **Write before you build.** Every new instruction from Emmanuel goes into this file on `main` as soon as he gives
   it, in his own words, before any code (the standing rule at the top). A documentation-only commit through
   `scripts/push-main.sh` is enough.
4. **Checkpoint early and often.** Update the checkpoint after every meaningful step: a new instruction, a task
   started or stopped, a branch pushed, a release, a blocker. Don't wait for a task to finish or for a warning. When
   work has been heavy, or tasks start stopping with "reached its limit", checkpoint at once: that is the sign the end
   is near. Work that only lives on your computer dies with you, so push unfinished work to a `wip/<name>` branch.
5. **Each checkpoint says, per item:** requested, queued, implemented, verified, blocked, remaining. Plus the task
   IDs, branches, worktrees and files, the next action, and what access the next Maya needs (which account and which
   sign-in step, section 2). Never a key, token or password.
6. **Be honest about interruptions.** Never write that unfinished work shipped, that tests passed when they didn't
   run, or that simulated users exist or are running when they don't. Queued is not running, built is not released,
   released is not verified. When you don't know, write "unknown, check".
7. **Hand over.** When Emmanuel says he's switching, or the limits keep coming, make a final checkpoint: statuses in
   section 0, the handover table below, one Log line, pushed to `main`. Tell him in the chat that it's saved, with the
   commit link. He doesn't have to do anything with it.
8. **Pass it on.** Keep this section in the file and keep it current, so Maya 7 inherits it from Maya 6 exactly as
   Maya 6 inherited it from Maya 5. Add to it, never drop anything. When Emmanuel tells you that you are Maya N, the
   one after you is Maya N+1.

**Seeing the limit coming, and handing over on your own (added Fri 23 Oct 2026, 09:20 CEST, by Maya 1).** Emmanuel,
voice message 09:18: "can you teach the other Mayas how to do the handoff procedure? I can't always have to teach them,
or tell them ... how you noticed in time to tell me and then prepared everything. Same mechanism for the other Mayas."
This is that mechanism. Every Maya runs it herself; Emmanuel never has to ask or explain.

*The warning signs (act on the first one, don't wait for a second):*
- A background task comes back **"Goal not met after 0 attempt(s) ... Stopped: this task reached its limit"**. Zero
  attempts means the account is out of task budget, not that the job was hard. Two of those in a row = you are at the
  limit now.
- Emmanuel says "we reached the limit", "switching to Maya N+1", or similar.
- Long stretches of heavy work (many releases, sims, coordinators) in one day: checkpoint anyway.

*What still works at the limit:* when tasks die, your own direct tool calls in the chat (exec, git, push, voice note)
usually still run for a while. So the handover is done **by you, directly, in the chat turn, never in a new task**
(a task started at the limit dies at 0 attempts, as on 23 Oct). Do it in as few calls as possible, most important first.

*The handover runbook (about 5 calls):*
0. **Rolling, before the limit (added Mon 26 Oct 2026, Maya 10):** the handover is no longer a one-off. Every coding
   task pushes its branch after each step, and every task and bug-hunt run ends with
   `bash /home/sprite/wt-harness/probes/sync-vm.sh "<what changed>"` (agent-harness branch): notes, probes, research and
   tooling go to `agent-harness`, unfinished worktrees to `wip/<branch>`, with a secret scan. The short handoff for the
   next Maya is `docs/HANDOFF-NEXT-MAYA.md` plus its PDF `docs/HANDOFF-NEXT-MAYA.pdf` (section 0.20). At the limit:
   run sync-vm.sh once more, refresh that file and PDF, then send Emmanuel his prompt word for word plus the PDF.
1. **Save everything on your computer:** `scripts/handover.sh <your N> <N+1>` from a fresh worktree of `origin/main`
   (`git worktree add -B handoff-mayaN1-<date> ../wt-handoffN1 origin/main`). It pushes every unfinished worktree to
   `wip/<branch>` and copies the work queue plus `OWNER-REQUESTS-*.md` into `handoff/queue-<date>/`, and prints a table.
2. **Make sure his latest words are in `OWNER-REQUESTS`** (the standing rule says this happens the moment he says
   something; check the last few messages anyway) before step 1 copies it.
3. **Write a new section 0.x "Handover Maya N -> Maya N+1"** above section 1, like 0.8: his words that triggered it,
   live build and `origin/main` SHA, "no task running" (or which), where the queue is, the release HOLD if any,
   abandoned queue claims to reclaim, the table from step 1 with honest states (built / released / verified / unknown),
   new requests not yet in the queue, and his delivery preferences. One Log line at the end.
4. **Push:** commit, then `flock /tmp/lowispike-release.lock scripts/push-main.sh handover-maya<N+1>`, and also push
   the branch itself (`git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/handoff-maya<N+1>-<date>`) so it survives even if CI is slow.
5. **Tell Emmanuel, once, as a voice note (his preference), short:** you're at the limit, the handover is saved on
   GitHub (section 0.x), and what to tell Maya N+1: "read MAYA-HANDOFF.md on main, start with section 0.x and
   OWNER-REQUESTS". No list of setbacks.
6. **New Maya, first thing:** read 0.6 and the newest 0.x handover section, copy `handoff/queue-<date>/` to your own
   `queue/`, reclaim the abandoned claims, recreate any schedules (hourly checklist, sweeps) on your own agent, and keep
   this runbook in the file for the Maya after you.

**The clone prompt for the next Maya stays in the chat only (Emmanuel, Sun 25 Oct 2026, about 11 PM): "I don't need it on GitHub."** **It is UNIVERSAL (Emmanuel, ~11:15 PM: "I wanted the universal prompt"): no Maya numbers, no dates, nothing to fill in.** The same text works for every Maya; each new Maya works out where she is from the newest "Handover" section. At handover, send him the exact prompt you were given, word for word and unchanged, right in the chat with the voice note. Send `docs/HANDOFF-NEXT-MAYA.pdf` with it (also to his Gmail): the prompt points here, this file points to 0.20, and 0.20 points to the PDF. Never put the prompt itself in this repo.

**Handover table.** Checkpoint by Maya 5, Monday 19 Oct 2026, 9:20 PM CEST. Live **20261019-201827**, `main` at
510f7f7 before this commit. Tasks run only on Maya 5's account and worktrees live only on her computer: a new Maya
resumes from the `wip/*` branches, not from them.

Nothing committed or released. `COORD-intuition.md` still says "Supabase blocked in harness". No simulated accounts confirmed: treat as none until you check Supabase. | `wip/intuition-playtest` (f29d9ec): snapshot of the untracked `tools/community-crowd/` (`crowd.cjs`, `content.cjs`, `meal-photos.json`), unverified | Section 0.5 item 18: solve Supabase access for test accounts, then run the crowd in stages |

Nothing released. | `wip/spanish-natural` (f51b0e8): snapshot of its uncommitted edits to 14 files plus `tests/spanish-natural.test.cjs` and `tools/es-natural-edits.py`, unverified | Run the tests, review the Spanish, release it |

### 0.7 Launch gate: run our own App Review-style check before real-money launch (recorded 22 Oct 2026, 7:50 AM CEST)

Emmanuel (call, 22 Oct about 7:30 AM): "Learn what Apple's verification process all would entail. And then remind me when it would be almost done to, you know, run our own version." Asked when: "Whatever you feel like, it's time to do so."

- **The checklist:** `LAUNCH_REVIEW.md` (researched from Apple's App Review Guidelines, last updated 16 Oct 2026, and Apple's App Review, account deletion, privacy label, accessibility label and EU pages). Ten areas with pass/fail lines: completeness and stability, honest and health-safe claims, privacy, account deletion, security and data access (Supabase row-level security, no secret keys, Stripe webhook-verified unlocks, affiliate codes), Community report/block/filter, permissions, payments and cancelling, accessibility, devices and network. None of it has been run yet.
- **The trigger (this is the reminder):** the first release that would let people pay real money for Plus through Stripe, or Emmanuel saying the app is ready to launch, whichever comes first. Whoever reaches that point: STOP before that release and tell Emmanuel in one short line, plus a voice note, that it's time to run our own review; then run `LAUNCH_REVIEW.md` in small tasks, one area each. The same rule is in `RELEASE.md` ("Launch gate"). Nothing watches the repo automatically and no dated reminder exists, so this only works if you read it.
- **Do not:** call LOWiSPIKE "Apple verified", add a self-made verified/beta badge, or describe App Review as a penetration test or proof that the app can't be hacked. Apple's own list says it "doesn't replace the guidelines or guarantee approval." Apple's commission is not one flat 30% (15% Small Business Program; EU terms since 1 Oct 2026 differ), and none of it applies to Stripe sales on the website. Easy cancelling is a legal and trust requirement, not a security measure.
- **Known blockers already:** item 29 real-iPhone Community -> chat crash (section 1 of the checklist fails until his phone confirms it); Stripe checkout, cancel button and webhook not built yet.
- **Privacy data list (what the app keeps about a person, for the privacy label and account deletion):** meal photos (since 22 Oct): a photo meal's photo is kept **on the phone only** (IndexedDB `lowi-meal-photos`, about 1024 px JPEG), and a 160 px thumbnail plus the full estimate (dish, foods, nutrition, spike rating, note) sit on the meal log, so they ride the private account backup (`user_state`, own row only). Nothing is public unless the user taps Share to Community for that one meal; then only that photo (public `media/<uid>/meal-<id>.jpg`), the dish name, the spike rating and their caption are posted, never weight or other health data. Deleting the account removes them: the device wipe drops `lowi-meal-photos`, and `delete-my-account` deletes `user_state`, the `media/<uid>/` files and the messages. A private Supabase bucket for meal photos (cross-device) is **not** set up yet: it needs a new bucket + RLS policy, Emmanuel's call.

fasting text** (cards, spike tips, scanner, tutorial, voice lines, creator scripts, bot posts, Cook, Shop). Read it before you
write such a text. In short: strict on sugar, flour, bread, rice, pasta, potatoes, juice, soft drinks; protein and non-starchy
veg first; no liquid sugar; sweets only as dessert; 10-minute walk helps (never "helps most"); vinegar only optional; no
small-portion tips for craving food; never diagnose, "remission" not "cure"; no keto/fasting/ketone/supplement healing claims.

re-recorded), herb spike tips (kidneys line out, vinegar optional), the scan card's "Eating it anyway?" line, the cauli_mash
swap. Guarded by tests/gap-meal-curve.test.cjs and tests/nutrition-policy.test.cjs. Do not bring the old lines back.

  before release (AI_PLAYBOOK section 10).

### 0.26 Microphone dictation in every text field (Emmanuel, voice, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 4:00 PM CET)

His words: "Can you add a speak thingy? For example, when I don't always want to type what food I wrote. So, I can click on
a microphone and then I can just speak and it writes it. Are we able to do that? And have that basically everywhere where
you're supposed to type, minimally."

What to build: one shared component (opt in with one attribute on any input/textarea), a small glass mic inside the field's
right edge. Tap, speak, the words land in the field; tap again or silence stops. Gentle listening pulse, big tap target, no
clutter. Food logging / food search / meal names first, then Scan search, Kitchen + shopping list add, Supermarket, captions,
companion rename and other free-text fields. Recognition in the app language (EN/DE/ES), every string EN/DE/ES. Permission
denied = short localized hint. Audio only ever leaves the phone after the user taps the mic. If it can't work somewhere, hide
the mic instead of showing a broken button. Task "LOWiSPIKE: microphone dictation in every text field" (Sesame, 26 Oct),
worktree wt-mic, branch mic-dictation-4, COORD-mic.md, HANDOFF-mic.md. Status: releasing (see Log for the build).
Cost decision (Emmanuel: "if it costs money, use the device's own built-in speech-to-text"): Google cloud speech-to-text
charges past a capped free tier, so the mic uses ONLY the device's free SpeechRecognition (webkitSpeechRecognition). No cloud
service, no keys, no Supabase voice-text function (an early draft had one; it was removed and never deployed). iOS blocks
that API inside Home Screen web apps (open WebKit bug), so there a tap focuses the field, opens the keyboard and a localized
hint points at the keyboard's own dictation mic. Auto-attaches to free-text fields >= 150 px; OPT_IN / OPT_OUT lists in
mic-dictate.js; data-mic / data-mic="off" per field. Chat composer: dictation mic inside the text box, the voice-message
mic next to Send is untouched. What's New: new-mic-dictation (voiced EN/DE/ES). Probes: tools/mic-dictate/*.cjs.

### 0.30 Better pick must be a better version of the SAME kind of food (Emmanuel, voice + 2 screenshots, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 4:07 PM CET)

What he saw: he scanned "Mandeln Stifte" (K-Classic slivered almonds). The scan card said Estimate / Moderate and its
"Better pick" was "Eggplants in Onion & Tomato..." (Paliria "Eggplants in Onion & Tomato Sauce", rated Lower).
His words: "Why is it recommending me eggplants and onion when I scanned almonds? Why didn't it just recommend me a better
version, an alternative of what I'm trying to find here? That's stupid."

Rule from now on: a Better pick (scan card, Shop camera, Log meal's Scan, the name card, the swaps after a catch) is only
ever a better version of the same kind of food with the same use: other almonds / nuts / seeds for almonds, a lower-sugar
yogurt for a yogurt, a whole-grain bread for a bread. Real products the app already holds only (prefer the user's
shop / country), never invented. Nothing of the same kind that is actually better: no Better pick at all. Never a random
unrelated food. No Better pick when the scanned item is already Lower. EN/DE/ES.
Root cause (reproduced in node): index.html nameSwap()'s last fallback took ANY community product rated ok whose coarse
catOf() category matched, and "other" is the catch-all, so almonds (other) got eggplant sauce (other). swapsHTML() (the
swaps after a manual catch) had the same cat match. Task "LOWiSPIKE: Better pick only suggests same-kind alternatives"
(Sesame, 26 Oct), worktree wt-better-pick, branch better-pick-same-kind, COORD-better-pick.md.
Status: LIVE 20261026-170447 (quiet, no notification). better-pick-core.js (LowiBetterPick: kind of food from the name EN/DE/ES, specific category tags, only the cereal/bread/drink coarse cats; which kinds may stand in for which; best real product of an allowed kind, rated strictly better, the one sold in the user's Supermarket region first) now drives nameSwap() (the Better pick on Scan, Shop camera and Log meal, all via LowiDex.swap) and swapsHTML(). The meal engine's idea must fit the kind too; a barcode product the app cannot place gets no Better pick. Already Lower: none. Products Shuffle, Supermarket and store-picks have no Better pick (checked, untouched). Tests: tests/better-pick-same-kind.test.cjs (7, 6 fail on the old code). WebKit iPhone 13 check tools/better-pick/check.cjs, live DE/EN/ES: scanned Mandeln Stifte never shows the eggplant sauce; with Walnusskerne in the app the pick is Walnusskerne; fruit yogurt -> Skyr natur. Open: scan-swap-core anchored() writes ideas like "Plain erdnüsse" / "Plain yogur fresa" from the scanned name (same kind, clumsy words). His phone showed almonds as Moderate; on the mocked real product the live card says Lower (the scan-why task owns that verdict).

### 0.31 Scanner card: small blood sugar graph, "how often" in numbers, a simple Why (Emmanuel, voice + 2 screenshots, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 4:07 PM CET)

Screenshots: Shop camera card for "Mandeln Stifte" (K-Classic slivered almonds) saying "ESTIMATE Moderate - Moderate
estimated blood sugar impact", and its Details (Usual portion 50 g / Whole pack 100 g, 2.1 g carbs, 8 g fibre per 100 g ...).
His words: "I'm not satisfied with this yet. I don't like how it looks... It says lower estimate, moderate estimate blood
sugar impact. Let's add a little graph in there, why are you not adding the graph? And when I click more details, it's
confusing. It says your portion... What I'm looking for: when it says moderate, what does that mean? Can I eat this every
day? Every week? Once, twice, three, four, five times a week? I need to know exactly how many times I can eat this. What
does moderate mean? Same with lower, does that mean I can eat this every day? And if it says no, then also why? But just a
little tap button to understand the reasoning, the text doesn't have to be there clunky. Maybe I tap Moderate and it
opens, or it has a Why button, I click the Why and it has a simple explanation that a 9-year-old and a 70-80-year-old
would understand."

What to build (extend the one shared scanner card from 0.23, shop-scan.js / shop-scan-core.js / shop-scan.css, no new card):

   "estimate, not a measurement", Reduce Motion honoured.
2. "How often" in plain numbers for every verdict at the shown portion. This REPLACES the 24 Oct "no frequency" rule
   (DECISIONS: SELTEN never says "now and then"): he now explicitly wants a number. General guide, not medical advice.
3. A small "Why?" tap target that opens a short plain explanation (what the word means, why this food, how often).
   Collapsed by default.
4. Check the almonds verdict: 2.1 g carbs per 50 g reads as low impact. Fix the cause if it is a bug, explain it otherwise.
   Shop finds "Caught today" said "Medium (estimate)" for the same product while the card said "Moderate": one word.
5. Details: "Your portion" must say plainly where the portion came from; calmer, more native card overall. EN/DE/ES.

### 0.32 The companion never goes exploring on her own (Emmanuel, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 4 PM CET, voice + screenshot)

  Today row says "Start next journey" and a tap starts it (that already existed).

  open with its finds, quietly, no sheet. Trips the user started keep running.

### 0.33 Community: the way back to Home disappears (Emmanuel, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 4 PM CET, voice + 2 screenshots)

Emmanuel, word for word: "Once we're in the Community section, the back button disappears. Going back to the home
disappears. Fix that." His screenshots (Ranking and Chat): only the "Community · 1 here now" pill, the moon and his picture;
the space at the top left where the round glass Home button (#secX, section-nav.js) sits is empty.

- Cause (reproduced live, WebKit iPhone 13, tools/community-home-btn/repro.cjs): section-nav.css hid the button with
  `html.sec-open.mf-on .sec-x{display:none}`, and section-nav.js blocked the edge swipe and Escape on `mf-on` too. `mf-on`
  means "your companion is flying". Flying only happens on Home, but autopilot keeps `mf-on` set after you leave Home
  (the flight sits in its exit state until you come back). So with your companion flying, every page (Community and all
  its tabs, Move, Cook/Shop, Profile) had no Home button, no swipe back and no Escape. No way home but the phone's back.
- Rule: a page (html.sec-open) always shows its Home button. Flying never hides it, because the flight is not on screen
  on a page. Only a real full-screen layer with its own close (camera b3-camon, herb room sp-open, a Profile part's Back
  pill) may hide it. The edge swipe and Escape follow the same rule.
- Tap target: Apple's 44 pt minimum. The glass circle stays 40 px; an invisible ring makes the tap area 48 px.
- Test: tests/section-back-while-flying.test.cjs.
- His iPad screenshot (German, Community > Chat) showed the button and his iPhone did not: not a width rule. Live WebKit at
  390x844, 430x932 and 820x1180 (tools/community-home-btn/width-probe.cjs): shown at every width while still, hidden at
  every width while flying. His iPhone had his companion on autopilot; the iPad did not.

What to build (extend existing code, no second version of anything):
- Evening greeting: after about 7 PM, or when the day has little or nothing logged, opening the app greets gently "Ready to

- Each pick pops the bubble with a burst, sparkles, points floating up, haptics where the app uses them; your companion
  cheers; next bubble drifts in. Points fair and in line with the reward table, no farming.
- End: read back the day in plain words, one big "Save". Entries saved at the approximate time of day (morning / noon /
  evening). Calm finish celebration, companion day/level updates.
- Accessibility for about 80 years old: very large text and targets, high contrast, few words, one thing per screen,
  VoiceOver labels, Reduce Motion calm version, one-handed. EN/DE/ES with recorded companion voice. "your companion", never
  "Misty". Honest wording, no medical promises.

  tap. A closed sheet can linger as .r2-ov.off for a few seconds in headless WebKit; open() then waits at most 2 s.

## 1. What LOWiSPIKE is

**The idea.** A mobile-first health and food web app (a PWA, added to the iPhone home screen) that turns everyday
metabolic habits into a living game. Emmanuel calls it a game, not a tracker: stats behave like character stats. You
care for a companion by doing real things (drinking water, logging meals, moving, sleeping, fasting gaps, scanning
food). Real actions are the only source of care and XP; spiky meals earn nothing, never minus.

**The companion.** A glass, water-like bubble creature living in a cell. **Misty** is the standard companion and the
fallback. A new account gets its own companion at onboarding: a birthday (and birth time) "reveal" computes a unique
look and a name (examples from today's tests: "Kreb Themis", "Selketlix"). Since build 20261019-155424 that revealed
companion is the one drawn on Home, in Forms, in the care room and in flight. The code still says `herb` in many places
(the companion was a potted herb until 16 Oct); `LowiHerbTamagotchi`, `herb-*.js` are that legacy.

**Energy.** `LowiSun.energy()` in `sun-meter.js`: base 0.15 + food 40% + water 30% + Move 30%. It drives her face
(thriving = short calm mouth, never a smile on Home), her flight speed and the world's pace and glow. A new account
gets 12 hours of welcome energy at 100%, fading over the next 12 (build 20261019-154834). The **half-energy rule**:
below half energy she bounces off worlds and cannot enter; leaving a world when weak, the rings can pull her back in.

**Home.** One page: Misty in the middle, eight same-size round glass bubbles on one circle (Settings on top, Scan lower
right, joystick at the bottom, plus Fuel, Hydration, Move, Gap, Sleep). Each bubble "blooms" its own choices around
her. No shadows on any bubble. The joystick is a plain white icon and vanishes while you steer.

**Gap (fasting).** The time since your last real meal. No known meal means no gap: the bubble shows a dash until the
first meal or the answer to "When did you last eat?" (fixes the fake 19-hour fast, build 20261019-154642).

**Fuel.** Shop, Cook and Log meal. Log meal understands typed meals through a built-in USDA meal database ("chicken
stew" is chicken stew, never "not provided"). Being built now: Breakfast/Lunch/Dinner/Snacks slots and nutrition
stats against daily targets.

**Scan and Snap food.** Scan reads barcodes (product card with verdict, details, cheaper stores) and Snap food
photographs a meal. The photo goes to the Supabase edge function `food-photo`, which asks Google Gemini Flash-Lite
(free tier) and returns dishes with portion, kcal, protein, carbs, sugar, fiber and fat. **Three photo checks per
person per day** (Snap food and Community share them; typing a meal is unlimited), counted on the server per account.

**Worlds.** A spirit world galaxy of everyone's bubbles; Misty flies out of her cell, rides glowing strings on
autopilot (Ant-Man camera), and dives into body-organ cell worlds (liver, lungs, ...) with cell "guides" who teach
real body science, remember what they told you, quiz you ("Remember?") and gossip about each other. Leaving a world
is an Interstellar-style rocket launch. Evolution: 25 levels since 22 Oct 2026 (section 8, Evolution plan).

**Community.** Profiles, friends tree, Care board (ranks sunbeams), meal-photo sharing with the AI analysis under the
photo (user reviews it before posting), a shoot-out mini game, and since build 20261019-163105 the **Interstellar
chat room**: tapping Chat opens a full-screen room in the tactical star-chart look (semi-transparent glass panels,
glowing accents, each person's colour, Today/Yesterday markers, recent voices row).

**Tutorial.** Settings > Tutorial replays a 14-card tour led by your own companion (Misty if you have none), with Back,
Skip and Skip all (builds 20261019-171941 and the flicker fix 20261019-172632). An automatic tour for new accounts
after setup is being released now. Its content lives only in `tutorial-steps.js`, and every release must keep it
current (section 3).

**Languages.** English, German and Spanish everywhere, including What's New.

**How the code is organized.** Static site, no build step, about 220 root files plus `tests/` (286 files, about
1,670 tests) and `data/`.

- `index.html` is the shell (build id, script includes, onboarding `finishOb()`), `sw.js` the service worker (cache
  name carries the build), `version.json` the live build plus the What's New entry.
- Biggest modules: `spirit.js` (screens, sheets, photo sheet, Today legacy), `misty-flight.js` (flight),
  `world-home.js` (Home world, adventure), `body-world-3d-scene.js` and `body-world-regions.js` (3D worlds, leaving
  a world), `community-chat.js` and `community-room.css` (chat room), `sun-meter.js` (energy), `cell-world.js`
  (companion cell drawing), `companion-birth-core.js` (the reveal), `region-pals*.js` (guides),
  `home-orbit-v2.js` (the ring), `home-blooms-v2.js`, `home-settings-sleep-v2.js` (Settings panel, Sleep),
  `daily-ritual.js` (the daily Day card), `food-photo-ai.js` and `meal-photo.js` (Snap food client),
  `tutorial.js` and `tutorial-steps.js`, `store-picks*.js` and `grocery-hunt.js` (Shop), `top-bar.js`.
- `*-core.js` files hold pure logic that the Node tests load directly.
- `supabase/functions/`: `food-photo`, `auth-handoff`, `owner-delete-account` (source). `supabase/proposals/`: SQL the
  owner has to run himself.
- `scripts/`: `release.sh`, `push-main.sh`, `wait-ci.cjs`, `bump-build.cjs`, `release-check.cjs`, `verify-live.cjs`,
  `worktrees-report.sh`.
- Root docs to read: `AGENTS.md` (rules for agents), `SUCCESSOR_HANDOFF.md` (state on 18 Oct), `DECISIONS.md`
  (every product decision, newest at the bottom, newest wins), `RELEASE.md`, `TESTING.md`, `DATA_CONTRACTS.md`
  (localStorage and cloud keys), `TUTORIAL.md`, `COMMUNITY_CHAT.md`, `CONTEXT.md` (older product context).

---

## 2. Accounts and access, step by step

### 0.37 Move session gave no points, no animation + make finishing a session the biggest reward with a grand celebration (Emmanuel, voice on iPad in German UI + screenshot, Mon 26 Oct 2026 about 6:14 PM CET)

His words: "I just finished a session here in the Move, and I didn't get any points. No animation, no congrats. This
should be giving me the most points ever. The most points that can be reached should be finishing one of those
movements. And then the animation should be grandiose."

Screenshot: Bewegen right after his session, still "Diese Woche 0/10 Min", "Verlauf: Noch keine", "Schritte heute auf
Home: noch nichts". So the finished session was not recorded at all: a bug, not only a missing reward.

What to build: (1) find and fix why a finished session is not saved/rewarded, for every Move session type (Spiegel-Modus,
Kreuzheben, Desk Spike Breaker, quick starts), signed in, iPad, German; (2) finishing a Move session pays more XP than any
other single action in the app, scaled by real minutes (5-60), anti-farm (must really run, daily cap); sunbeams/Ranking stay
consistent; (3) a grand full-screen finish celebration (companion celebrates, reward count-up, light burst in the glass/cosmic
style, haptics, "Geschafft!" with minutes and points), smooth, tap to dismiss, calm under Reduce Motion, companion's own name,
EN/DE/ES; (4) Diese Woche, Verlauf and the companion's day update right away.
Task "LOWiSPIKE: Move session gives no reward + grand finish celebration" (Sesame, 26 Oct), worktree wt-move-reward, branch
move-reward, COORD-move-reward.md, continued by "finish + ship Move session reward + grand finish (continue)".
Status: LIVE in 20261026-190121 (main e7fd027, quiet, What's New tour step `new-move-finish`). Live WebKit check after the
release: tools/move-finish/proof.cjs on the live site, iPhone 13 EN/DE, iPhone 13 Reduce Motion, iPad upright ES: 68/68.
What was wrong: Mirror mode and Kreuzheben only fired `lowi:mirror-done`, which nothing listened to, so they were never saved
(no Diese Woche, no Verlauf, no XP). The Move player saved but paid at most 30 XP (`Math.min(30, 2 * min)`).
What it does now: mirror-move.js finish() -> `MOVE.record({fmt:'mirror'|'hinge', moved: real seconds of follow/rest,
complete:true})` -> index.html saveSession + sessionReward + celebrateSession (one path with the Move player).
Reward (move-finish-core.js): 150 + 5 per real minute, up to 60 minutes (450 max), 3 paid sessions a day (`move:session`,
eventId per session, quiet award). Counts from 3 minutes, or from 1 real minute when the routine ran to its own end
(`complete`): Kreuzheben is about 3 minutes and skipping a rest makes it shorter, so it must never pay nothing again.
The 4th session of a day still saves and celebrates with "no more points today". Grand finish: move-finish.js/.css,
full-screen, companion's own name, EN/DE/ES, Reduce Motion calm, Back closes it, its line in the recorded voice
(tools/app-voice/phrases.cjs source `move-finish`). Tests: tests/move-finish.test.cjs (9, all fail on the old code);
tests/xp-tap.test.cjs health caps moved to the core. Proof: tools/move-finish/proof.cjs (Kreuzheben 130 s -> 160 XP,
Desk 10 min -> 200 XP, shows in Verlauf, own name, covers the screen, no errors) on iPhone 13, iPhone 13 Reduce Motion,
iPhone 13 German, iPad upright Spanish, iPad sideways German and Chrome 390: 102/102 checks.
Not checked on Emmanuel's real iPhone/iPad yet.

### GitHub

- Account: **therealetm** (Emmanuel's). Repository: **github.com/therealetm/lowispike**, public. Branch `main` is live.
- Live site: **https://therealetm.github.io/lowispike/** (GitHub Pages, source "GitHub Actions").
- Actions: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs "Release check and tests" on every push, then on `main` deploys Pages and
  waits until the live site serves the build. `keepalive.yml` only pings Supabase every three days. A commit message
  containing `[skip ci]` skips CI (use it for non-app branches).
- Ruleset "main: tests must pass": nothing lands on `main` unless "Release check and tests" passed on that exact
  commit; force pushes and deleting main are blocked, nobody can bypass. That is why releases go through
  `scripts/push-main.sh` (section 3).
- History from his Gmail: on 9 Oct he created a classic token "LOWiSPIKE deploy" and authorized the Supabase OAuth app
  on GitHub; on 17 Oct an SSH deploy key named "Maya" was added to the repo; on 18 Oct evening the GitHub CLI was
  signed in with a device code. Those credentials stay on the old computer and do not travel to you.

**How you get GitHub access (the "GitHub thingy" Emmanuel mentioned).** Use the GitHub CLI device-code sign-in:

1. On your computer: `gh auth login --hostname github.com --git-protocol https --web`. It prints a one-time code
   (8 characters, like ABCD-1234) and the address https://github.com/login/device.
2. Post that code and the address to Emmanuel **right in the chat** (not only in the activity feed), in one short
   message: "Open github.com/login/device, enter ABCD-1234, tap Authorize." He approves "GitHub CLI" for therealetm.
   The code expires after about 15 minutes; if it does, run it again and send the new code.
3. Then `gh auth setup-git` and check `gh auth status` (the old token had scopes repo, workflow, gist, read:org).
4. Clone: `git clone https://github.com/therealetm/lowispike.git /home/sprite/lowispike` (keep this path: scripts
   and probes expect it; or adjust them).

Never ask him to paste a token or password in chat. If GitHub asks him to confirm a sign-in from a new place, that
email goes to his Gmail; tell him it is you.

### Supabase (the backend, where the data is saved)

- Emmanuel's Supabase account was made with **"Continue with GitHub"**, using his GitHub account (same email as
  GitHub). He has no separate Supabase password. He was confused by this once ("I only ever used GitHub"): explain it
  simply as "Supabase is the app's database; you log into it with your GitHub button."
- Project: **lowispike**, ref **vnfwmifmsfecascwaody**, region eu-central-1, created 9 Oct 2026. Dashboard:
  https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/vnfwmifmsfecascwaody. The app talks to
  `https://vnfwmifmsfecascwaody.supabase.co` with the public publishable key that is already in the client code.
- Edge functions deployed (checked 19 Oct, 7 PM): `food-photo` (version 6), `auth-handoff` (version 4), `n1-push`
  (version 4), all ACTIVE with verify_jwt off (they check the session themselves). The source of `n1-push` (push
  notifications) is NOT in the repo; download it from the dashboard before changing it. `owner-delete-account` is in
  the repo but not deployed.
- Secrets, **names only**: `GEMINI_API_KEY` (Google AI Studio key for food photos, added by Emmanuel on 19 Oct),
  `N1_VAPID_JWK`, `N1_VAPID_PUB`, `N1_CRON_KEY` (push notifications), and Supabase's own `SUPABASE_URL`,
  `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEYS`, `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS`,
  `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, `SUPABASE_JWKS`. Optional `GEMINI_MODEL` overrides the model chain. Never read or print a value.
- How the dashboard sign-in worked on 19 Oct: the agent opened supabase.com in its cloud browser, pressed "Continue
  with GitHub", and GitHub was already signed in as therealetm, so no code was needed. Then it deployed `food-photo`
  through the Supabase Management API with a personal access token that stayed on the old computer
  (`~/.supabase/access-token`, never in the repo). You need your own: sign the cloud browser in the same way (if
  GitHub is not signed in, ask Emmanuel for his GitHub login through the secure vault form, or show him the live view
  so he signs in and approves any GitHub email or 2FA prompt himself), then create a token under Account > Access
  Tokens and keep it only on your computer, or deploy from the dashboard.
- Emmanuel finds the Supabase editor too complicated on his phone. Do dashboard steps yourself in the cloud browser.
  The one thing he does himself is typing a secret value (for example a new Gemini key) into Edge Functions > Secrets,
  or into the live view while you watch. If he ever pastes a key in chat, tell him to delete that key and make a new
  one (this happened once on 19 Oct).
- Admin-only work (deleting users, changing policies, running `supabase/proposals/*.sql`) is his call: prepare it,
  ask in chat, then do it. Two test users are left on the server from 19 Oct tests (one guest from the photo-cap test,
  id starting 3392f841, and one empty guest from the food-photo test); he may delete them under Authentication.

### Gmail and the other tools

- His Gmail (sesame.email app) holds the setup trail: Supabase welcome and the GitHub OAuth approval (9 Oct), the
  "LOWiSPIKE deploy" token (9 Oct), the SSH key "Maya" (17 Oct), GitHub sign-in alerts. Search it before asking him.
- Voice notes: the sesame.voice_notes app records them; send each one into the chat with its checklist.
- Cloud browser: `sesame_browser__browse` for dashboards; show him the live view when he must act.
- The hourly bug hunt runs as a Sesame schedule (`0 * * * *`, Europe/Berlin) with the custom skill "LOWiSPIKE hourly
  bug hunt". Its message: play the game hard like a paid QA tester, fix what breaks, keep the bug memory, hunt for
  siblings, pick up from the ledger and HANDOFF after a limit, and only message Emmanuel when something shipped or
  needs him (voice note plus checklist). If you are on a new agent, recreate it the same way.

## 3. How to work

### Understand first

Emmanuel's rule: understand the whole app before changing code. Read `AGENTS.md`, this file, the `DECISIONS.md`
entries for the area, and the files you will touch. Reproduce a bug before fixing it.

### Worktrees and COORD files (parallel tasks)

Several tasks ran at once. Each one:

1. Makes its own worktree from fresh `origin/main`: `git -C /home/sprite/lowispike worktree add
   /home/sprite/wt-<name> -b <name> origin/main`.
2. Writes `/home/sprite/COORD-<name>.md` first: task name, branch, worktree, the exact files it claims, then a Status
   list it appends to with times ("18:15 started", "18:55 tests 1676/1676, releasing", "RELEASED build ...").
3. Reads the other COORD files before touching a shared file; when two tasks need the same file, one waits for the
   other's release and then rebases. Notes to another task go in that task's COORD file.
4. **Appends only.** On 19 Oct one task rewrote a COORD file and wiped another task's section. Never rewrite.
5. Writes a `HANDOFF-<name>.md` with what it learned, so a retry after a budget stop can continue.
6. When something ships, breaks or is cancelled, appends one line to the **Log** at the end of this file (same commit
   as the release when possible).

Old COORD and HANDOFF files are on the `agent-harness` branch under `vm-notes/`. `scripts/worktrees-report.sh` lists
every worktree with merged and dirty state.

### Release procedure

**What's New = mini tour (Oct 23 2026, Emmanuel).** Every user-visible release ships a tour step: a step in part `new` of `tutorial-steps.js` (EN/DE/ES, short, voice recorded) and news.json `"tour": ["<step id>"]`. The update opens that place and points at the new thing in the tutorial card ("Überspringen" any time). Only one announcement per launch, all unseen releases folded in, seen saved before it shows. Text-only What's New is gone; a pure fix (`"tutorial": {"none": ...}`) shows one card in the same style. Details: `TUTORIAL.md`, "What's New is a mini tour". Live check: `node tools/check-whats-new-tour.cjs https://therealetm.github.io/lowispike/ iphone` (and `ipad`).

```bash
# from the worktree, with the change committed
scripts/release.sh "what users get, in one line" --news news.json
```

- `news.json` is the What's New entry: `{"slug": "...", "go": {"tab": "home"}, "text": {"en": "...", "de": "...",
  "es": "..."}, "tutorial": "<step id>"}`. Plain words, what the user notices, all three languages. `"tutorial"` must
  name the step in `tutorial-steps.js` that explains the change, or be `{"none": "why"}`; the release is refused
  otherwise (rule since the tutorial release). A new Home ring control or Settings row needs a tutorial step or a
  `NO_TUTORIAL` reason, or `tests/tutorial-steps.test.cjs` fails.
- What the script does: takes the lock `/tmp/lowispike-release.lock`, fetches, rebases on `origin/main` (stops on a
  conflict), bumps the build id (format `YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS`, in `index.html`, `version.json`, `sw.js`, and
  `apple-ui.js` when needed), runs `scripts/release-check.cjs` and all tests, checks whitespace, commits, lands it
  with `scripts/push-main.sh` (pushes to a throwaway `ci/...` branch, waits for CI on that exact commit, then a plain
  fast-forward push to `main`, never force), frees the lock, and waits for the live site with
  `scripts/verify-live.cjs --at-least --compare` (index.html, sw.js and version.json show the build and the live bytes
  match the commit).
- If `main` moved while you waited, the fast-forward is refused: re-run `release.sh`; it rebases. Never force.
- Documentation-only commits need no new build id, but still land through `push-main.sh` (CI must pass).
- GitHub's own deploy step can show "waiting" or stay queued even when the live site already serves the build (it
  happened 19 Oct; a newer release deploying on top supersedes it). Trust `verify-live`, not the badge. On 19 Oct
  from about 6 PM a GitHub Actions incident kept CI queued for a long time; releases simply waited.
- Pages caches for 10 minutes; always fetch with a cache-buster or use `verify-live.cjs`.

### Testing

- Full suite: `node --test tests/*.test.cjs` (about 1,670 tests on 19 Oct). Every release must pass all of them.
- Every fix gets new tests, and at least one of them must **fail on the old code** (say how many in the report).
  Never remove or loosen a test to get green.
- Browser checks with Playwright (version 1.64.1, installed in `/tmp/pw`, WebKit and Chromium): simulated iPhone 13
  (WebKit), iPad portrait and landscape (WebKit), Chrome at 390x844, Reduce Motion, English/German/Spanish. The
  probes are on the `agent-harness` branch (`probes/BUGHUNT/playtest/lib.cjs` and the `probe-*.cjs` files; setup in
  that branch's README). Some probes live in the repo under `tests/*-live.cjs` and `playtest/`.
- **Safe testing:** a browser test against the live site must block the production Supabase host or use a local test
  sign-in, and must not create real profiles, messages or activity. On 17 to 18 Oct test profiles ("eman", "QA")
  landed on Emmanuel's Care board. When a real-server test is unavoidable (food-photo), say so and name the test user.
  Quick /tmp scripts count too: on 26 Oct two of them (bug hunt run 31, /tmp/fsvis.cjs + /tmp/amod.cjs, no route at all)
  put a fake "F" (Moss Drifter) at 1st on the new week's board, and /tmp/bh26-r2.cjs left five "E" guests (0.14). Since
  release 20261026-055906 every guest made by an automated browser (navigator.webdriver) signs up with lowi_test:true and is
  profiles.test, hidden from real people; that is the safety net, not a licence: still block writes unless a live write is the test.
- Reports must say what kind of evidence it is: unit tests, simulated browser, live site, or Emmanuel's real phone.
  You never have his phone; say "simulated iPhone 13, not your phone" and give him one exact thing to try.
- Under heavy machine load some test files fail as a whole (herb-tamagotchi, ui-joystick-fade, ui-settings-panel)
  and pass alone or on retry. Kill leftover test browsers from finished tasks before a full run.

## 4. Lessons and difficulties (so you don't go through them again)

- Container query units (cqw/cqh) on the container element itself resolve against the ancestor container or the viewport, not itself. Put container-type on the parent. Measuring spacing missed this for a whole release; look at the frames (screenshot + OCR) before calling a visual done.

1. **Tasks running out of budget.** Many tasks on 19 Oct stopped before releasing: "three food photos" (three runs,
   started already over budget; it had in fact released, and round 2 only verified it), "meal names" and the
   old-design purge (released, but the task failed before its report), the chat room (released, report incomplete),
   and six tasks that stopped at their limit before doing anything (Snap food fix, chat room keyboard rounds 2 and 3,
   iPad chat room layout, everyone gets their own companion, first replay-tutorial attempt). How to avoid it:
   - One small goal per task: one feature, one release. Split "build + test + release + live check + voice note".
   - Write COORD and HANDOFF status early and often, so a fresh task can continue from the file.
   - Release as soon as tests pass; polish in a second task.
   - Don't run too many tasks at once; on 19 Oct around ten in parallel exhausted the shared budget and new ones died
     at birth. A failed task's work usually still sits in its worktree: check git and the live build before redoing.
   - Retrying a task that is over budget does nothing. Start a fresh task that names the branch and HANDOFF file.
2. **Supabase confusion.** Emmanuel did not know he had a Supabase account; it was made with "Continue with GitHub".
   Finding which account held the project took a long back-and-forth and a Gmail search. Now you know: project
   lowispike, ref vnfwmifmsfecascwaody, signed in with GitHub.
3. **Gemini key.** The food-photo feature needed `GEMINI_API_KEY` as a Supabase secret. Emmanuel once pasted a key in
   chat (it had to be replaced). When food photos fail, check the key first (deleted or replaced key = every photo
   fails), then the function logs, then the daily cap.
4. **Crashes from heavy effects.** iPhone and iPad crashed when flying in and out of worlds: each exit kept a
   full-size copy of the 3D canvas until memory ran out (fixed 20261019-142709: small copy, freed at once). Earlier,
   observer write loops and per-frame rewrites caused heat and crashes (18 Oct). Rule since: new effects only adjust
   lights and values that already exist; no new meshes, lights or textures per frame; free canvases; test long runs.
   The autopilot froze once (bug hunt round 1). A Community shoot-out crash is being fixed now.
5. **Overlay bugs (things covering other things).** A recurring class: Misty's praise line and Home notes covering
   bubbles, the guide's bubble on the joystick, the Move note on the Move bubble, the update bubble over setup, the
   daily Day card staying on top of setup and every later panel (BH-008, 20261019-160738), and the tutorial flicker
   (it read the Day card's "wait for" list, found itself on it and hid, about three times a second; fixed
   20261019-172632). Rule: anything that sits on top must yield to what opens after it, never count itself, and never
   cover a ring bubble, the joystick or a choice. When you find one, check every sibling overlay.
6. **The fake 19-hour fast.** An old Today fasting module still ran on every screen and invented "last meal 8 PM
   yesterday" when none was saved; at 3 PM that is 19 hours, and "I just ate" even paid points for it. Fixed
   20261019-154642. Lesson: legacy modules keep running in the background; search for them.
7. **Companions wiped.** `companion.js` wrote Community settings into the same storage key as the companion record
   (`lowispike.companion.v1`), so the companion was wiped seconds after it was brought to life (BH-006/007, fixed
   20261019-143513; 422 of 440 cloud backups were affected). Records lost before about 2:35 PM on 19 Oct cannot be
   restored; the user can bring the companion to life again in Profile with the same birthday and birth time and gets
   the same look. Home also called her by the old herb name "Moss Leaf".
8. **Snap food stopped working on Emmanuel's iPad** (19 Oct, about 4:19 PM, "I couldn't read this photo", plus the
   photo sheet buttons overlapping on iPad). Not the limit. The fix task stopped at its limit; what it found: the
   edge function logs show the iPad never reached `food-photo`; his iPhone did 8 successful reads from 3:45 to
   3:57 PM, then a GET with no POST at 4:11 PM. So the failure is on the client side (session token wait / request
   never sent / layout). Its half-done work is on `wip/snap-fix`. Still open.
9. **Release collisions.** With many tasks releasing, `main` moves under you; the fast-forward is refused and you
   re-run `release.sh`. Hold the lock only while landing. Build each release on the live build so nothing is
   reverted.
10. **Test data in production** (see Testing). 11. **Old docs disagree with new ones:** `CONTEXT.md` describes the
   herb era, and `SUCCESSOR_HANDOFF.md` says "no voice recordings"; both are superseded (section 5). The newest
   DECISIONS entry wins. 12. **A note can be wrong:** the bug hunt ledger says old-design purge round 2 was never
   released, but git shows rounds 1 to 4 all live (20261019-134704, 135912, 141244, 142359). Check git and the live
   site before believing a note.
